It’s not like I’m calmly deciding, “Okay, I want more food now.” It feels more automatic than that. I can know I should stop and still keep going.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty immediately after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “immediately” every time. That word makes me hesitate, because sometimes right after it happens I’m kind of numb, like I’m just sitting there and realizing what I did, but I’m not even fully feeling it yet. It’s more like this blank feeling at first, like… okay, that just happened.\n\nThen the guilt can come in after, but I’m not sure exactly how fast. Sometimes it’s pretty soon, like once I see the wrappers or the empty containers or I notice how much is gone, then I start feeling really bad about myself. But other times I think I’m more dazed than guilty at first, if that makes sense.\n\nSo I guess I can’t honestly say I usually feel guilty immediately. I do feel bad afterward a lot of the time, but the timing is kind of messy. It’s not always like the second I stop eating I’m hit with guilt. Sometimes it takes a little while for my head to catch up.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to control what you're eating during these episodes, even if you want to stop?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes. That’s probably the clearest way to describe it, honestly. It’s not just that I’m eating more than I meant to, it’s that once I’ve started, it feels like I don’t have control of it anymore.\n\nLike, once I take that first bite, something in me kind of clicks off. I can still hear myself thinking, you know, “Demetrius, stop. You don’t need any more. This is enough.” I’ll be telling myself that while it’s happening, not just afterward when I’m looking back on it. But even with that thought going through my head, my hands just keep reaching for the next thing, and then the next thing.\n\nIt’s a really strange feeling, because part of me is there and knows I want to stop, but another part is just… doing it anyway. It doesn’t feel like a normal choice in that moment. It feels like I’m watching myself keep going even though I already decided I should stop.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever try to make up for these eating episodes by purging, using laxatives, or excessively exercising afterward?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that, because I don’t think of myself as doing a specific “make up for it” thing in that way. Like, I know what you mean with purging or laxatives or exercising really hard afterward, but… I don’t know. That’s not something I’ve really noticed as part of what happens after.\n\nUsually after one of those episodes I’m more just sitting with the aftermath, if that makes sense. I don’t have a clear routine where I go, “Okay, now I have to do this to undo it.” I might have thoughts about wanting it not to have happened, but as far as those specific things you named, I don’t have a solid yes to give you.\n\nI guess if you’re asking whether I regularly throw up, take laxatives, or go exercise for hours because of it, I don’t think so. At least not in a way that I’d recognize as a pattern.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, not like making myself throw up on purpose after it happens. I’ve definitely felt sick or overly full afterward sometimes, like my stomach is uncomfortable and I’m sitting there regretting it, but I don’t go to the bathroom and force myself to vomit or anything like that.\n\nI guess I want to be careful how I answer, because there have been times I’ve thought, like, “I wish I could just undo this,” but that’s more a thought or a feeling of wanting it gone, not me actually doing that. I don’t have a pattern of making myself vomit after those episodes.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever use laxatives after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. Not in that way.\n\nI mean, I know what laxatives are, obviously, and I understand why you’re asking, but after those episodes I don’t go take them to try to clear everything out or undo what I ate. That’s not really something I’ve done as a response to it.\n\nAfterward I’m usually more just uncomfortable and upset with myself, and kind of stuck in my head about what happened. But I’m not reaching for laxatives or planning around that or anything. So for that specific question, I’d say no, I don’t use laxatives after.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever exercise excessively after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean… I’ve had moments where I think, “I should do something, I should go walk this off,” or I feel guilty and restless afterward, but I wouldn’t say I go into some intense workout or exercise for hours to try to cancel it out.\n\nMost of the time after it happens, I honestly feel too heavy and uncomfortable to do much of anything. I might pace around a little or tell myself I should be more active tomorrow, but it’s not like I immediately go running or force myself through a workout because of what I ate.\n\nSo, no, I don’t think I exercise excessively after those episodes. It’s more that I sit with the guilt and discomfort than try to undo it that way."
  }
